===== M0359 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.handleBreak(Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 551-599
Documentation:

Method code:
551 |   private void handleBreak(Node node) {
552 |     String label = null;
553 |     // See if it is a break with label.
554 |     if (node.hasChildren()) {
555 |       label = node.getFirstChild().getString();
556 |     }
557 |     Node cur;
558 |     Node previous = null;
559 |     Node lastJump;
560 |     Node parent = node.getParent();
561 |     /*
562 |      * Continuously look up the ancestor tree for the BREAK target or the target
563 |      * with the corresponding label and connect to it. If along the path we
564 |      * discover a FINALLY, we will connect the BREAK to that FINALLY. From then
565 |      * on, we will just record the control flow changes in the finallyMap. This
566 |      * is due to the fact that we need to connect any node that leaves its own
567 |      * FINALLY block to the outer FINALLY or the BREAK's target but those nodes
568 |      * are not known yet due to the way we traverse the nodes.
569 |      */
570 |     for (cur = node, lastJump = node;
571 |         !isBreakTarget(cur, label);
572 |         cur = parent, parent = parent.getParent()) {
573 |       if (cur.isTry() && NodeUtil.hasFinally(cur)
574 |           && cur.getLastChild() != previous) {
575 |         if (lastJump == node) {
576 |           createEdge(lastJump, Branch.UNCOND, computeFallThrough(
577 |               cur.getLastChild()));
578 |         } else {
579 |           finallyMap.put(lastJump, computeFallThrough(cur.getLastChild()));
580 |         }
581 |         lastJump = cur;
582 |       }
583 |       if (parent == null) {
584 |         if (compiler.isIdeMode()) {
585 |           // In IDE mode, we expect that the data flow graph may
586 |           // not be well-formed.
587 |           return;
588 |         } else {
589 |           throw new IllegalStateException("Cannot find break target.");
590 |         }
591 |       }
592 |       previous = cur;
593 |     }
594 |     if (lastJump == node) {
595 |       createEdge(lastJump, Branch.UNCOND, computeFollowNode(cur, this));
596 |     } else {
597 |       finallyMap.put(lastJump, computeFollowNode(cur, this));
598 |     }
599 |   }

===== M0369 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.JSType.checkEquivalenceHelper(JSType,EquivalenceMethod)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/JSType.java
Original range: 571-639
Documentation:
/**
   * An equivalence visitor.
   */
Method code:
571 |   boolean checkEquivalenceHelper(JSType that, EquivalenceMethod eqMethod) {
572 |     if (this == that) {
573 |       return true;
574 |     }
575 | 
576 |     boolean thisUnknown = isUnknownType();
577 |     boolean thatUnknown = that.isUnknownType();
578 |     if (thisUnknown || thatUnknown) {
579 |       if (eqMethod == EquivalenceMethod.INVARIANT) {
580 |         // If we're checking for invariance, the unknown type is invariant
581 |         // with everyone.
582 |         return true;
583 |       } else if (eqMethod == EquivalenceMethod.DATA_FLOW) {
584 |         // If we're checking data flow, then two types are the same if they're
585 |         // both unknown.
586 |         return thisUnknown && thatUnknown;
587 |       } else if (thisUnknown && thatUnknown &&
588 |           (isNominalType() ^ that.isNominalType())) {
589 |         // If they're both unknown, but one is a nominal type and the other
590 |         // is not, then we should fail out immediately. This ensures that
591 |         // we won't unbox the unknowns further down.
592 |         return false;
593 |       }
594 |     }
595 | 
596 |     if (isUnionType() && that.isUnionType()) {
597 |       return toMaybeUnionType().checkUnionEquivalenceHelper(
598 |           that.toMaybeUnionType(), eqMethod);
599 |     }
600 | 
601 |     if (isFunctionType() && that.isFunctionType()) {
602 |       return toMaybeFunctionType().checkFunctionEquivalenceHelper(
603 |           that.toMaybeFunctionType(), eqMethod);
604 |     }
605 | 
606 |     if (isRecordType() && that.isRecordType()) {
607 |       return toMaybeRecordType().checkRecordEquivalenceHelper(
608 |           that.toMaybeRecordType(), eqMethod);
609 |     }
610 | 
611 |     if (!getTemplateTypeMap().checkEquivalenceHelper(
612 |         that.getTemplateTypeMap(), eqMethod)) {
613 |       return false;
614 |     }
615 | 
616 |     if (isNominalType() && that.isNominalType()) {
617 |       return toObjectType().getReferenceName().equals(
618 |           that.toObjectType().getReferenceName());
619 |     }
620 | 
621 |     // Unbox other proxies.
622 |     if (this instanceof ProxyObjectType) {
623 |       return ((ProxyObjectType) this)
624 |           .getReferencedTypeInternal().checkEquivalenceHelper(
625 |               that, eqMethod);
626 |     }
627 | 
628 |     if (that instanceof ProxyObjectType) {
629 |       return checkEquivalenceHelper(
630 |           ((ProxyObjectType) that).getReferencedTypeInternal(),
631 |           eqMethod);
632 |     }
633 | 
634 |     // Relies on the fact that for the base {@link JSType}, only one
635 |     // instance of each sub-type will ever be created in a given registry, so
636 |     // there is no need to verify members. If the object pointers are not
637 |     // identical, then the type member must be different.
638 |     return this == that;
639 |   }

===== M0379 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.ObjectType.isUnknownType()
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/ObjectType.java
Original range: 549-569
Documentation:
/**
   * We treat this as the unknown type if any of its implicit prototype
   * properties is unknown.
   */
Method code:
549 |   @Override
550 |   public boolean isUnknownType() {
551 |     // If the object is unknown now, check the supertype again,
552 |     // because it might have been resolved since the last check.
553 |     if (unknown) {
554 |       ObjectType implicitProto = getImplicitPrototype();
555 |       if (implicitProto == null ||
556 |           implicitProto.isNativeObjectType()) {
557 |         unknown = false;
558 |         for (ObjectType interfaceType : getCtorExtendedInterfaces()) {
559 |           if (interfaceType.isUnknownType()) {
560 |             unknown = true;
561 |             break;
562 |           }
563 |         }
564 |       } else {
565 |         unknown = implicitProto.isUnknownType();
566 |       }
567 |     }
568 |     return unknown;
569 |   }

===== M0381 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.graph.GraphReachability.GraphReachability(DiGraph<N, E>,Predicate<EdgeTuple<N, E>>)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/graph/GraphReachability.java
Original range: 51-55
Documentation:
/**
   * @param graph The graph.
   * @param edgePredicate Given the predecessor P of the a node S and the edge

===== M0395 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.graph.FixedPointGraphTraversal.computeFixedPoint(DiGraph<N, E>,Set<N>)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/graph/FixedPointGraphTraversal.java
Original range: 92-127
Documentation:
/**
   * Compute a fixed point for the given graph, entering from the given nodes.
   * @param graph The graph to traverse.
   * @param entrySet The nodes to begin traversing from.
   */
Method code:
92 |   public void computeFixedPoint(DiGraph<N, E> graph, Set<N> entrySet) {
93 |     int cycleCount = 0;
94 |     long nodeCount = graph.getNodes().size();
95 | 
96 |     // Choose a bail-out heuristically in case the computation
97 |     // doesn't converge.
98 |     long maxIterations = Math.max(nodeCount * nodeCount * nodeCount, 100);
99 | 
100 |     // Use a LinkedHashSet, so that the traversal is deterministic.
101 |     LinkedHashSet<DiGraphNode<N, E>> workSet =
102 |         Sets.newLinkedHashSet();
103 |     for (N n : entrySet) {
104 |       workSet.add(graph.getDirectedGraphNode(n));
105 |     }
106 |     for (; !workSet.isEmpty() && cycleCount < maxIterations; cycleCount++) {
107 |       // For every out edge in the workSet, traverse that edge. If that
108 |       // edge updates the state of the graph, then add the destination
109 |       // node to the resultSet, so that we can update all of its out edges
110 |       // on the next iteration.
111 |       DiGraphNode<N, E> source = workSet.iterator().next();
112 |       N sourceValue = source.getValue();
113 | 
114 |       workSet.remove(source);
115 | 
116 |       List<DiGraphEdge<N, E>> outEdges = source.getOutEdges();
117 |       for (DiGraphEdge<N, E> edge : outEdges) {
118 |         N destNode = edge.getDestination().getValue();
119 |         if (callback.traverseEdge(sourceValue, edge.getValue(), destNode)) {
120 |           workSet.add(edge.getDestination());
121 |         }
122 |       }
123 |     }
124 | 
125 |     Preconditions.checkState(cycleCount != maxIterations,
126 |         NON_HALTING_ERROR_MSG);
127 |   }

===== M0399 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.CodeGenerator.addNonEmptyStatement(Node,Context,boolean)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/CodeGenerator.java
Original range: 826-882
Documentation:
/**
   * Adds a block or expression, substituting a VOID with an empty statement.
   * This is used for "for (...);" and "if (...);" type statements.
   *
   * @param n The node to print.
   * @param context The context to determine how the node should be printed.
   */
Method code:
826 |   private void addNonEmptyStatement(
827 |       Node n, Context context, boolean allowNonBlockChild) {
828 |     Node nodeToProcess = n;
829 | 
830 |     if (!allowNonBlockChild && !n.isBlock()) {
831 |       throw new Error("Missing BLOCK child.");
832 |     }
833 | 
834 |     // Strip unneeded blocks, that is blocks with <2 children unless
835 |     // the CodePrinter specifically wants to keep them.
836 |     if (n.isBlock()) {
837 |       int count = getNonEmptyChildCount(n, 2);
838 |       if (count == 0) {
839 |         if (cc.shouldPreserveExtraBlocks()) {
840 |           cc.beginBlock();
841 |           cc.endBlock(cc.breakAfterBlockFor(n, context == Context.STATEMENT));
842 |         } else {
843 |           cc.endStatement(true);
844 |         }
845 |         return;
846 |       }
847 | 
848 |       if (count == 1) {
849 |         // Hack around a couple of browser bugs:
850 |         //   Safari needs a block around function declarations.
851 |         //   IE6/7 needs a block around DOs.
852 |         Node firstAndOnlyChild = getFirstNonEmptyChild(n);
853 |         boolean alwaysWrapInBlock = cc.shouldPreserveExtraBlocks();
854 |         if (alwaysWrapInBlock || isOneExactlyFunctionOrDo(firstAndOnlyChild)) {
855 |           cc.beginBlock();
856 |           add(firstAndOnlyChild, Context.STATEMENT);
857 |           cc.maybeLineBreak();
858 |           cc.endBlock(cc.breakAfterBlockFor(n, context == Context.STATEMENT));
859 |           return;
860 |         } else {
861 |           // Continue with the only child.
862 |           nodeToProcess = firstAndOnlyChild;
863 |         }
864 |       }
865 | 
866 |       if (count > 1) {
867 |         context = Context.PRESERVE_BLOCK;
868 |       }
869 |     }
870 | 
871 |     if (nodeToProcess.isEmpty()) {
872 |       cc.endStatement(true);
873 |     } else {
874 |       add(nodeToProcess, context);
875 | 
876 |       // VAR doesn't include ';' since it gets used in expressions - so any
877 |       // VAR in a statement context needs a call to endStatement() here.
878 |       if (nodeToProcess.isVar()) {
879 |         cc.endStatement();
880 |       }
881 |     }
882 |   }

===== M0402 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.UnreachableCodeElimination.EliminationPass.removeDeadExprStatementSafely(Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/UnreachableCodeElimination.java
Original range: 190-238
Documentation:

Method code:
190 |     private void removeDeadExprStatementSafely(Node n) {
191 |       Node parent = n.getParent();
192 |       if (n.isEmpty() || (n.isBlock() && !n.hasChildren())) {
193 |         // Not always trivial to remove, let FoldConstants work its magic later.
194 |         return;
195 |       }
196 | 
197 |       // TODO(user): This is a problem with removeNoOpStatements.
198 |       // Every expression in a FOR-IN header looks side effect free on its own.
199 |       if (NodeUtil.isForIn(parent)) {
200 |         return;
201 |       }
202 | 
203 |       switch (n.getType()) {
204 |         // Removing an unreachable DO node is messy b/c it means we still have
205 |         // to execute one iteration. If the DO's body has breaks in the middle,
206 |         // it can get even more tricky and code size might actually increase.
207 |         case Token.DO:
208 |           return;
209 | 
210 |         case Token.BLOCK:
211 |           // BLOCKs are used in several ways including wrapping CATCH
212 |           // blocks in TRYs
213 |           if (parent.isTry() && NodeUtil.isTryCatchNodeContainer(n)) {
214 |             return;
215 |           }
216 |           break;
217 | 
218 |         case Token.CATCH:
219 |           Node tryNode = parent.getParent();
220 |           NodeUtil.maybeAddFinally(tryNode);
221 |           break;
222 |       }
223 | 
224 |       if (n.isVar() && !n.getFirstChild().hasChildren()) {
225 |         // Very unlikely case, Consider this:
226 |         // File 1: {throw 1}
227 |         // File 2: {var x}
228 |         // The node var x is unreachable in the global scope.
229 |         // Before we remove the node, redeclareVarsInsideBranch
230 |         // would basically move var x to the beginning of File 2,
231 |         // which resulted in zero changes to the AST but triggered
232 |         // reportCodeChange().
233 |         // Instead, we should just ignore dead variable declarations.
234 |         return;
235 |       }
236 | 
237 |       removeNode(n);
238 |     }

===== M0403 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.UnreachableCodeElimination.process(Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/UnreachableCodeElimination.java
Original range: 64-85
Documentation:

Method code:
64 |   @Override
65 |   public void process(Node externs, Node toplevel) {
66 |     NodeTraversal.traverseChangedFunctions(compiler, new FunctionCallback() {
67 |         @Override
68 |         public void visit(AbstractCompiler compiler, Node root) {
69 |           // Computes the control flow graph.
70 |           ControlFlowAnalysis cfa =
71 |               new ControlFlowAnalysis(compiler, false, false);
72 |           cfa.process(null, root);
73 |           ControlFlowGraph<Node> cfg = cfa.getCfg();
74 |           new GraphReachability<Node, ControlFlowGraph.Branch>(cfg)
75 |               .compute(cfg.getEntry().getValue());
76 |           if (root.isFunction()) {
77 |             root = root.getLastChild();
78 |           }
79 |           do {
80 |             codeChanged = false;
81 |             NodeTraversal.traverse(compiler, root, new EliminationPass(cfg));
82 |           } while (codeChanged);
83 |         }
84 |       });
85 |   }

Original range: 654-691
Documentation:
/**
   * Converts a tree to JS code
   */
Method code:
654 |   private static String toSource(Node root, Format outputFormat,
655 |       CompilerOptions options, JSTypeRegistry registry,
656 |       SourceMap sourceMap,  boolean tagAsStrict) {
657 |     Preconditions.checkState(options.sourceMapDetailLevel != null);
658 | 
659 |     boolean createSourceMap = (sourceMap != null);
660 |     MappedCodePrinter mcp =
661 |         outputFormat == Format.COMPACT
662 |         ? new CompactCodePrinter(
663 |             options.lineBreak,
664 |             options.preferLineBreakAtEndOfFile,
665 |             options.lineLengthThreshold,
666 |             createSourceMap,
667 |             options.sourceMapDetailLevel)
668 |         : new PrettyCodePrinter(
669 |             options.lineLengthThreshold,
670 |             createSourceMap,
671 |             options.sourceMapDetailLevel);
672 |     CodeGenerator cg =
673 |         outputFormat == Format.TYPED
674 |         ? new TypedCodeGenerator(mcp, options, registry)
675 |         : new CodeGenerator(mcp, options);
676 | 
677 |     if (tagAsStrict) {
678 |       cg.tagAsStrict();
679 |     }
680 | 
681 |     cg.add(root);
682 |     mcp.endFile();
683 | 
684 |     String code = mcp.getCode();
685 | 
686 |     if (createSourceMap) {
687 |       mcp.generateSourceMap(sourceMap);
688 |     }
689 | 
690 |     return code;
691 |   }

   * @param item The item to inspect

   */
Method code:
794 |   private PropListItem removeProp(PropListItem item, int propType) {

===== M0464 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.NodeTraversal.traverseChangedFunctions(AbstractCompiler,FunctionCallback)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/NodeTraversal.java
Original range: 463-481
Documentation:
/**
   * Traversal for passes that work only on changed functions.
   * Suppose a loopable pass P1 uses this traversal.
   * Then, if a function doesn't change between two runs of P1, it won't look at
   * the function the second time.
   * (We're assuming that P1 runs to a fixpoint, o/w we may miss optimizations.)
   */
Method code:
463 |   public static void traverseChangedFunctions(
464 |       AbstractCompiler compiler, FunctionCallback callback) {
465 |     final AbstractCompiler comp = compiler;
466 |     final FunctionCallback cb = callback;
467 |     final Node jsRoot = comp.getJsRoot();
468 |     if (comp.hasScopeChanged(jsRoot)) {
469 |       cb.visit(comp, jsRoot);
470 |     }
471 |     traverse(comp, jsRoot,
472 |         new AbstractPreOrderCallback() {
473 |           @Override
474 |           public final boolean shouldTraverse(NodeTraversal t, Node n, Node p) {
475 |             if (n.isFunction() && comp.hasScopeChanged(n)) {
476 |               cb.visit(comp, n);
477 |             }
478 |             return true;
479 |           }
480 |         });
481 |   }

===== M0466 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.handleReturn(Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 639-666
Documentation:

Method code:
639 |   private void handleReturn(Node node) {
640 |     Node lastJump = null;
641 |     for (Iterator<Node> iter = exceptionHandler.iterator(); iter.hasNext();) {
642 |       Node curHandler = iter.next();
643 |       if (curHandler.isFunction()) {
644 |         break;
645 |       }
646 |       if (NodeUtil.hasFinally(curHandler)) {
647 |         if (lastJump == null) {
648 |           createEdge(node, Branch.UNCOND, curHandler.getLastChild());
649 |         } else {
650 |           finallyMap.put(lastJump,
651 |               computeFallThrough(curHandler.getLastChild()));
652 |         }
653 |         lastJump = curHandler;
654 |       }
655 |     }
656 | 
657 |     if (node.hasChildren()) {
658 |       connectToPossibleExceptionHandler(node, node.getFirstChild());
659 |     }
660 | 
661 |     if (lastJump == null) {
662 |       createEdge(node, Branch.UNCOND, null);

Method code:
1721 |   static Node getConditionExpression(Node n) {
1722 |     switch (n.getType()) {
1723 |       case Token.IF:

1725 |         return n.getFirstChild();

   * Reduce the alternates into a non-union type.
   * If the alternates can't be accurately represented with a non-union

288 |   private JSType reduceAlternatesWithoutUnion() {

/**
   * Given an entry node, find all the nodes reachable from that node
   * and prioritize them.
   */
Method code:
196 |   private void prioritizeFromEntryNode(DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> entry) {
197 |     PriorityQueue<DiGraphNode<Node, Branch>> worklist =
198 |         new PriorityQueue<DiGraphNode<Node, Branch>>(10, priorityComparator);
199 |     worklist.add(entry);
200 | 
201 |     while (!worklist.isEmpty()) {
202 |       DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> current = worklist.remove();
203 |       if (nodePriorities.containsKey(current)) {
204 |         continue;
205 |       }
206 |

208 | 
209 |       List<DiGraphNode<Node, Branch>> successors =
210 |           cfg.getDirectedSuccNodes(current);
211 |       for (DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> candidate : successors) {
212 |         worklist.add(candidate);
213 |       }
214 |     }
215 |   }

Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.NodeUtil.precedence(int)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/NodeUtil.java
Original range: 1170-1245
Documentation:
/*
   *  0 comma ,
   *  1 assignment = += -= *= /= %= <<= >>= >>>= &= ^= |=
   *  2 conditional ?:
   *  3 logical-or ||
   *  4 logical-and &&
   *  5 bitwise-or |
   *  6 bitwise-xor ^
   *  7 bitwise-and &
   *  8 equality == !=
   *  9 relational < <= > >=
   * 10 bitwise shift << >> >>>
   * 11 addition/subtraction + -
   * 12 multiply/divide * / %
   * 13 negation/increment ! ~ - ++ --
   * 14 call, member () [] .
   */
Method code:
1170 |   static int precedence(int type) {
1171 |     switch (type) {
1172 |       case Token.COMMA:  return 0;
1173 |       case Token.ASSIGN_BITOR:
1174 |       case Token.ASSIGN_BITXOR:
1175 |       case Token.ASSIGN_BITAND:
1176 |       case Token.ASSIGN_LSH:
1177 |       case Token.ASSIGN_RSH:
1178 |       case Token.ASSIGN_URSH:
1179 |       case Token.ASSIGN_ADD:
1180 |       case Token.ASSIGN_SUB:
1181 |       case Token.ASSIGN_MUL:
1182 |       case Token.ASSIGN_DIV:
1183 |       case Token.ASSIGN_MOD:
1184 |       case Token.ASSIGN: return 1;
1185 |       case Token.HOOK:   return 2;  // ?: operator
1186 |       case Token.OR:     return 3;
1187 |       case Token.AND:    return 4;
1188 |       case Token.BITOR:  return 5;
1189 |       case Token.BITXOR: return 6;
1190 |       case Token.BITAND: return 7;
1191 |       case Token.EQ:
1192 |       case Token.NE:
1193 |       case Token.SHEQ:
1194 |       case Token.SHNE:   return 8;
1195 |       case Token.LT:
1196 |       case Token.GT:
1197 |       case Token.LE:
1198 |       case Token.GE:
1199 |       case Token.INSTANCEOF:
1200 |       case Token.IN:     return 9;
1201 |       case Token.LSH:
1202 |       case Token.RSH:
1203 |       case Token.URSH:   return 10;
1204 |       case Token.SUB:
1205 |       case Token.ADD:    return 11;
1206 |       case Token.MUL:
1207 |       case Token.MOD:
1208 |       case Token.DIV:    return 12;
1209 |       case Token.INC:
1210 |       case Token.DEC:
1211 |       case Token.NEW:

1214 |       case Token.VOID:
1215 |       case Token.NOT:

1219 |

1224 |       case Token.ARRAYLIT:

===== M0481 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.NodeTraversal.traverseFunction(Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/NodeTraversal.java
Original range: 539-573
Documentation:
/** Traverses a function. */
Method code:
539 |   private void traverseFunction(Node n, Node parent) {
540 |     Preconditions.checkState(n.getChildCount() == 3);
541 |     Preconditions.checkState(n.isFunction());
542 | 
543 |     final Node fnName = n.getFirstChild();
544 | 
545 |     boolean isFunctionExpression = (parent != null)
546 |         && NodeUtil.isFunctionExpression(n);
547 | 
548 |     if (!isFunctionExpression) {
549 |       // Functions declarations are in the scope containing the declaration.
550 |       traverseBranch(fnName, n);
551 |     }
552 | 
553 |     curNode = n;
554 |     pushScope(n);
555 | 
556 |     if (isFunctionExpression) {
557 |       // Function expression names are only accessible within the function
558 |       // scope.
559 |       traverseBranch(fnName, n);
560 |     }
561 | 
562 |     final Node args = fnName.getNext();
563 |     final Node body = args.getNext();
564 | 
565 |     // Args
566 |     traverseBranch(args, n);
567 | 
568 |     // Body
569 |     Preconditions.checkState(body.getNext() == null && body.isBlock(), body);
570 |     traverseBranch(body, n);
571 | 
572 |     popScope();
573 |   }

===== M0486 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.UnionTypeBuilder.addAlternate(JSType)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/UnionTypeBuilder.java
Original range: 120-281
Documentation:
/**
   * Adds an alternate to the union type under construction. Returns this
   * for easy chaining.
   */
Method code:
120 |   UnionTypeBuilder addAlternate(JSType alternate) {
121 |     // build() returns the bottom type by default, so we can
122 |     // just bail out early here.
123 |     if (alternate.isNoType()) {
124 |       return this;
125 |     }
126 | 
127 |     isAllType = isAllType || alternate.isAllType();
128 | 
129 |     boolean isAlternateUnknown = alternate instanceof UnknownType;
130 |     isNativeUnknownType = isNativeUnknownType || isAlternateUnknown;
131 |     if (isAlternateUnknown) {
132 |       areAllUnknownsChecked = areAllUnknownsChecked &&
133 |           alternate.isCheckedUnknownType();
134 |     }
135 |     if (!isAllType && !isNativeUnknownType) {
136 |       if (alternate.isUnionType()) {
137 |         UnionType union = alternate.toMaybeUnionType();
138 |         for (JSType unionAlt : union.getAlternates()) {
139 |           addAlternate(unionAlt);
140 |         }
141 |       } else {
142 |         if (alternates.size() > maxUnionSize) {
143 |           return this;
144 |         }
145 | 
146 |         // Function types are special, because they have their
147 |         // own bizarre sub-lattice. See the comments on
148 |         // FunctionType#supAndInf helper and above at functionTypePosition.
149 |         if (alternate.isFunctionType() && functionTypePosition != -1) {
150 |           // See the comments on functionTypePosition above.
151 |           FunctionType other =
152 |               alternates.get(functionTypePosition).toMaybeFunctionType();
153 |           FunctionType supremum =
154 |               alternate.toMaybeFunctionType().supAndInfHelper(other, true);
155 |           alternates.set(functionTypePosition, supremum);
156 |           result = null;
157 |           return this;
158 |         }
159 | 
160 |         // Look through the alternates we've got so far,
161 |         // and check if any of them are duplicates of
162 |         // one another.
163 |         int currentIndex = 0;
164 |         Iterator<JSType> it = alternates.iterator();
165 |         while (it.hasNext()) {
166 |           boolean removeCurrent = false;
167 |           JSType current = it.next();
168 | 
169 |           // Unknown and NoResolved types may just be names that haven't
170 |           // been resolved yet. So keep these in the union, and just use
171 |           // equality checking for simple de-duping.
172 |           if (alternate.isUnknownType() ||
173 |               current.isUnknownType() ||
174 |               alternate.isNoResolvedType() ||
175 |               current.isNoResolvedType() ||
176 |               alternate.hasAnyTemplateTypes() ||
177 |               current.hasAnyTemplateTypes()) {
178 |             if (alternate.isEquivalentTo(current)) {
179 |               // Alternate is unnecessary.
180 |               return this;
181 |             }
182 |           } else {
183 | 
184 |             // Because "Foo" and "Foo.<?>" are roughly equivalent
185 |             // templatized types, special care is needed when building the
186 |             // union. For example:
187 |             //   Object is consider a subtype of Object.<string>
188 |             // but we want to leave "Object" not "Object.<string>" when
189 |             // building the subtype.
190 |             //
191 | 
192 |             if (alternate.isTemplatizedType() || current.isTemplatizedType()) {
193 |               // Cases:
194 |               // 1) alternate:Array.<string> and current:Object ==> Object
195 |               // 2) alternate:Array.<string> and current:Array ==> Array
196 |               // 3) alternate:Object.<string> and
197 |               //    current:Array ==> Array|Object.<string>
198 |               // 4) alternate:Object and current:Array.<string> ==> Object
199 |               // 5) alternate:Array and current:Array.<string> ==> Array
200 |               // 6) alternate:Array and
201 |               //    current:Object.<string> ==> Array|Object.<string>
202 |               // 7) alternate:Array.<string> and
203 |               //    current:Array.<number> ==> Array.<?>
204 |               // 8) alternate:Array.<string> and
205 |               //    current:Array.<string> ==> Array.<string>
206 |               // 9) alternate:Array.<string> and
207 |               //    current:Object.<string> ==> Object.<string>|Array.<string>
208 | 
209 |               if (!current.isTemplatizedType()) {
210 |                 if (alternate.isSubtype(current)) {
211 |                   // case 1, 2
212 |                   return this;
213 |                 }
214 |                 // case 3: leave current, add alternate
215 |               } else if (!alternate.isTemplatizedType()) {
216 |                 if (current.isSubtype(alternate)) {
217 |                   // case 4, 5
218 |                   removeCurrent = true;
219 |                 }
220 |                 // case 6: leave current, add alternate
221 |               } else {
222 |                 Preconditions.checkState(current.isTemplatizedType()
223 |                     && alternate.isTemplatizedType());
224 |                 TemplatizedType templatizedAlternate = alternate.toMaybeTemplatizedType();
225 |                 TemplatizedType templatizedCurrent = current.toMaybeTemplatizedType();
226 | 
227 |                 if (templatizedCurrent.wrapsSameRawType(templatizedAlternate)) {
228 |                   if (alternate.getTemplateTypeMap().checkEquivalenceHelper(
229 |                       current.getTemplateTypeMap(),
230 |                       EquivalenceMethod.IDENTITY)) {
231 |                     // case 8
232 |                     return this;
233 |                   } else {
234 |                     // TODO(johnlenz): should we leave both types?
235 |                     // case 7: add a merged alternate
236 |                     // We currently merge to the templatized types to "unknown"
237 |                     // which is equivalent to the raw type.
238 |                     JSType merged = templatizedCurrent
239 |                         .getReferencedObjTypeInternal();
240 |                     return addAlternate(merged);
241 |                   }
242 |                 }
243 |                 // case 9: leave current, add alternate
244 |               }
245 |               // Otherwise leave both templatized types.
246 |             } else if (alternate.isSubtype(current)) {
247 |               // Alternate is unnecessary.
248 |               return this;
249 |             } else if (current.isSubtype(alternate)) {
250 |               // Alternate makes current obsolete
251 |               removeCurrent = true;
252 |             }
253 |           }
254 | 
255 |           if (removeCurrent) {
256 |             it.remove();
257 | 
258 |             if (currentIndex == functionTypePosition) {
259 |               functionTypePosition = -1;
260 |             } else if (currentIndex < functionTypePosition) {
261 |               functionTypePosition--;
262 |               currentIndex--;
263 |             }
264 |           }
265 |           currentIndex++;
266 |         }
267 | 
268 |         if (alternate.isFunctionType()) {
269 |           // See the comments on functionTypePosition above.
270 |           Preconditions.checkState(functionTypePosition == -1);
271 |           functionTypePosition = alternates.size();
272 |         }
273 | 
274 |         alternates.add(alternate);
275 |         result = null; // invalidate the memoized result
276 |       }
277 |     } else {
278 |       result = null;
279 |     }
280 |     return this;
281 |   }

===== M0504 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.Node.toString(StringBuilder,boolean,boolean,boolean)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/Node.java
Original range: 944-1003
Documentation:

Method code:
944 |   private void toString(
945 |       StringBuilder sb,
946 |       boolean printSource,
947 |       boolean printAnnotations,
948 |       boolean printType) {
949 |     sb.append(Token.name(type));
950 |     if (this instanceof StringNode) {
951 |       sb.append(' ');
952 |       sb.append(getString());
953 |     } else if (type == Token.FUNCTION) {
954 |       sb.append(' ');
955 |       // In the case of JsDoc trees, the first child is often not a string
956 |       // which causes exceptions to be thrown when calling toString or
957 |       // toStringTree.
958 |       if (first == null || first.getType() != Token.NAME) {
959 |         sb.append("<invalid>");
960 |       } else {
961 |         sb.append(first.getString());
962 |       }
963 |     } else if (type == Token.NUMBER) {
964 |       sb.append(' ');
965 |       sb.append(getDouble());
966 |     }
967 |     if (printSource) {
968 |       int lineno = getLineno();
969 |       if (lineno != -1) {
970 |         sb.append(' ');
971 |         sb.append(lineno);
972 |       }
973 |     }
974 | 
975 |     if (printAnnotations) {
976 |       int[] keys = getSortedPropTypes();
977 |       for (int i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {
978 |         int type = keys[i];
979 |         PropListItem x = lookupProperty(type);
980 |         sb.append(" [");
981 |         sb.append(propToString(type));
982 |         sb.append(": ");
983 |         String value;
984 |         switch (type) {
985 |           default:
986 |             value = x.toString();
987 |             break;
988 |         }
989 |         sb.append(value);
990 |         sb.append(']');
991 |       }
992 |     }
993 | 
994 |     if (printType) {
995 |       if (jsType != null) {
996 |         String jsTypeString = jsType.toString();
997 |         if (jsTypeString != null) {
998 |           sb.append(" : ");
999 |           sb.append(jsTypeString);
1000 |         }
1001 |       }
1002 |     }
1003 |   }

===== M0511 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.CodeConsumer.addNumber(double)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/CodeConsumer.java
Original range: 239-275
Documentation:

Method code:
239 |   void addNumber(double x) {
240 |     // This is not pretty printing. This is to prevent misparsing of x- -4 as
241 |     // x--4 (which is a syntax error).
242 |     char prev = getLastChar();
243 |     boolean negativeZero = isNegativeZero(x);
244 |     if ((x < 0 || negativeZero) && prev == '-') {
245 |       add(" ");
246 |     }
247 | 
248 |     if (negativeZero) {
249 |       addConstant("-0");
250 |     } else if ((long) x == x) {
251 |       long value = (long) x;
252 |       long mantissa = value;
253 |       int exp = 0;
254 |       if (Math.abs(x) >= 100) {
255 |         while (mantissa / 10 * Math.pow(10, exp + 1) == value) {
256 |           mantissa /= 10;
257 |           exp++;
258 |         }
259 |       }
260 |       if (exp > 2) {
261 |         addConstant(Long.toString(mantissa) + "E" + Integer.toString(exp));
262 |       } else {
263 |         long valueAbs = Math.abs(value);
264 |         if (Long.toHexString(valueAbs).length() + 2 <
265 |             Long.toString(valueAbs).length()) {
266 |           addConstant((value < 0 ? "-" : "") + "0x" +
267 |               Long.toHexString(valueAbs));
268 |         } else {
269 |           addConstant(Long.toString(value));
270 |         }
271 |       }
272 |     } else {
273 |       addConstant(String.valueOf(x).replace(".0E", "E"));
274 |     }
275 |   }

Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.Node.mergeLineCharNo(int,int)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/Node.java
Original range: 1191-1199
Documentation:
/**
   * Merges the line number and character number in one integer. The Character
   * number takes the first 12 bits and the line number takes the rest. If
   * the character number is greater than <code>2<sup>12</sup>-1</code> it is
   * adjusted to <code>2<sup>12</sup>-1</code>.
   */
Method code:
1191 |   protected static int mergeLineCharNo(int lineno, int charno) {
1192 |     if (lineno < 0 || charno < 0) {
1193 |       return -1;
1194 |     } else if ((charno & ~COLUMN_MASK) != 0) {
1195 |       return lineno << COLUMN_BITS | COLUMN_MASK;
1196 |     } else {
1197 |       return lineno << COLUMN_BITS | (charno & COLUMN_MASK);
1198 |     }
1199 |   }

===== M0527 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.process(Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 148-190
Documentation:

Method code:
148 |   @Override
149 |   public void process(Node externs, Node root) {
150 |     this.root = root;
151 |     astPositionCounter = 0;
152 |     astPosition = Maps.newHashMap();
153 |     nodePriorities = Maps.newHashMap();
154 |     cfg = new AstControlFlowGraph(computeFallThrough(root), nodePriorities,
155 |                                   edgeAnnotations);
156 |     NodeTraversal.traverse(compiler, root, this);
157 |     astPosition.put(null, ++astPositionCounter); // the implicit return is last.
158 | 
159 |     // Now, generate the priority of nodes by doing a depth-first
160 |     // search on the CFG.
161 |     priorityCounter = 0;
162 |     DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> entry = cfg.getEntry();
163 |     prioritizeFromEntryNode(entry);
164 | 
165 |     if (shouldTraverseFunctions) {
166 |       // If we're traversing inner functions, we need to rank the
167 |       // priority of them too.
168 |       for (DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> candidate : cfg.getDirectedGraphNodes()) {
169 |         Node value = candidate.getValue();
170 |         if (value != null && value.isFunction()) {
171 |           Preconditions.checkState(
172 |               !nodePriorities.containsKey(candidate) || candidate == entry);
173 |           prioritizeFromEntryNode(candidate);
174 |         }
175 |       }
176 |     }
177 | 
178 |     // At this point, all reachable nodes have been given a priority, but
179 |     // unreachable nodes have not been given a priority. Put them last.
180 |     // Presumably, it doesn't really matter what priority they get, since
181 |     // this shouldn't happen in real code.
182 |     for (DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> candidate : cfg.getDirectedGraphNodes()) {
183 |       if (!nodePriorities.containsKey(candidate)) {
184 |         nodePriorities.put(candidate, ++priorityCounter);
185 |       }
186 |     }
187 | 
188 |     // Again, the implicit return node is always last.
189 |     nodePriorities.put(cfg.getImplicitReturn(), ++priorityCounter);
190 |   }

===== M0529 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.NodeUtil.functionCallHasSideEffects(Node,AbstractCompiler)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/NodeUtil.java
Original range: 995-1052
Documentation:
/**
   * Returns true if calls to this function have side effects.
   *
   * @param callNode The call node to inspected.
   * @param compiler A compiler object to provide program state changing
   *     context information. Can be null.
   */
Method code:
995 |   static boolean functionCallHasSideEffects(
996 |       Node callNode, @Nullable AbstractCompiler compiler) {
997 |     if (!callNode.isCall()) {
998 |       throw new IllegalStateException(
999 |           "Expected CALL node, got " + Token.name(callNode.getType()));
1000 |     }
1001 | 
1002 |     if (callNode.isNoSideEffectsCall()) {
1003 |       return false;
1004 |     }
1005 | 
1006 |     Node nameNode = callNode.getFirstChild();
1007 | 
1008 |     // Built-in functions with no side effects.
1009 |     if (nameNode.isName()) {
1010 |       String name = nameNode.getString();
1011 |       if (BUILTIN_FUNCTIONS_WITHOUT_SIDEEFFECTS.contains(name)) {
1012 |         return false;
1013 |       }
1014 |     } else if (nameNode.isGetProp()) {
1015 |       if (callNode.hasOneChild()
1016 |           && OBJECT_METHODS_WITHOUT_SIDEEFFECTS.contains(
1017 |                 nameNode.getLastChild().getString())) {
1018 |         return false;
1019 |       }
1020 | 
1021 |       if (callNode.isOnlyModifiesThisCall()
1022 |           && evaluatesToLocalValue(nameNode.getFirstChild())) {
1023 |         return false;
1024 |       }
1025 | 
1026 |       // Math.floor has no side-effects.
1027 |       // TODO(nicksantos): This is a terrible terrible hack, until
1028 |       // I create a definitionProvider that understands namespacing.
1029 |       if (nameNode.getFirstChild().isName()) {
1030 |         if ("Math.floor".equals(nameNode.getQualifiedName())) {
1031 |           return false;
1032 |         }
1033 |       }
1034 | 
1035 |       if (compiler != null && !compiler.hasRegExpGlobalReferences()) {
1036 |         if (nameNode.getFirstChild().isRegExp()
1037 |             && REGEXP_METHODS.contains(nameNode.getLastChild().getString())) {
1038 |           return false;
1039 |         } else if (nameNode.getFirstChild().isString()
1040 |             && STRING_REGEXP_METHODS.contains(
1041 |                 nameNode.getLastChild().getString())) {
1042 |           Node param = nameNode.getNext();
1043 |           if (param != null &&
1044 |               (param.isString() || param.isRegExp())) {
1045 |             return false;
1046 |           }
1047 |         }
1048 |       }
1049 |     }
1050 | 
1051 |     return true;
1052 |   }

===== M0531 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.NodeUtil.isFunctionExpression(Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/NodeUtil.java
Original range: 1945-1947
Documentation:
/**
   * Is a FUNCTION node an function expression? An function expression is one
   * that has either no name or a name that is not added to the current scope.
   *
   * <p>Some examples of function expressions:
   * <pre>
   * (function () {})
   * (function f() {})()
   * [ function f() {} ]
   * var f = function f() {};
   * for (function f() {};;) {}
   * </pre>
   *
   * <p>Some examples of functions that are <em>not</em> expressions:
   * <pre>
   * function f() {}
   * if (x); else function f() {}
   * for (;;) { function f() {} }
   * </pre>
   *
   * @param n A node
   * @return Whether n is an function used within an expression.
   */
Method code:
1945 |   static boolean isFunctionExpression(Node n) {
1946 |     return n.isFunction() && !isStatement(n);
1947 |   }

===== M0538 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.handleCase(Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 451-471
Documentation:

Method code:
451 |   private void handleCase(Node node) {
452 |     // Case is a bit tricky....First it goes into the body if condition is true.
453 |     createEdge(node, Branch.ON_TRUE,
454 |         node.getFirstChild().getNext());
455 |     // Look for the next CASE, skipping over DEFAULT.
456 |     Node next = getNextSiblingOfType(node.getNext(), Token.CASE);
457 |     if (next != null) { // Found a CASE
458 |       Preconditions.checkState(next.isCase());
459 |       createEdge(node, Branch.ON_FALSE, next);
460 |     } else { // No more CASE found, go back and search for a DEFAULT.
461 |       Node parent = node.getParent();
462 |       Node deflt = getNextSiblingOfType(
463 |         parent.getFirstChild().getNext(), Token.DEFAULT_CASE);
464 |       if (deflt != null) { // Has a DEFAULT
465 |         createEdge(node, Branch.ON_FALSE, deflt);
466 |       } else { // No DEFAULT found, go to the follow of the SWITCH.
467 |         createEdge(node, Branch.ON_FALSE, computeFollowNode(node, this));
468 |       }
469 |     }
470 |     connectToPossibleExceptionHandler(node, node.getFirstChild());
471 |   }

Method code:
545 |   private void validateSwitch(Node n) {
546 |     validateNodeType(Token.SWITCH, n);
547 |     validateMinimumChildCount(n, 1);
548 |     validateExpression(n.getFirstChild());
549 |     int defaults = 0;
550 |     for (Node c = n.getFirstChild().getNext(); c != null; c = c.getNext()) {
551 |       validateSwitchMember(n.getLastChild());
552 |       if (c.isDefaultCase()) {
553 |         defaults++;
554 |       }
555 |     }
556 |     if (defaults > 1) {
557 |       violation("Expected at most 1 'default' in switch but was "
558 |           + defaults, n);
559 |     }
560 |   }

===== M0541 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.CodeConsumer.add(String)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/CodeConsumer.java
Original range: 180-203
Documentation:

Method code:
180 |   void add(String newcode) {
181 |     maybeEndStatement();
182 | 
183 |     if (newcode.length() == 0) {
184 |       return;
185 |     }
186 | 
187 |     char c = newcode.charAt(0);
188 |     if ((isWordChar(c) || c == '\\') &&
189 |         isWordChar(getLastChar())) {
190 |       // need space to separate. This is not pretty printing.
191 |       // For example: "return foo;"
192 |       append(" ");
193 |     } else if (c == '/' && getLastChar() == '/') {
194 |       // Do not allow a forward slash to appear after a DIV.
195 |       // For example,
196 |       // REGEXP DIV REGEXP
197 |       // is valid and should print like
198 |       // / // / /
199 |       append(" ");
200 |     }
201 | 
202 |     append(newcode);
203 |   }

94 |   public boolean addVarArgs(JSType type) {
95 |     if (hasVarArgs()) {

1077 |     Node processSwitchCase(SwitchCase caseNode) {
1078 |       Node node;
1079 |       if (caseNode.isDefault()) {
1080 |         node = newNode(Token.DEFAULT_CASE);
1081 |       } else {
1082 |         AstNode expr = caseNode.getExpression();
1083 |         node = newNode(Token.CASE, transform(expr));

===== M0548 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.CodeGenerator.unrollBinaryOperator(Node,int,String,Context,Context,int,int)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/CodeGenerator.java
Original range: 765-781
Documentation:
/**
   * We could use addList recursively here, but sometimes we produce
   * very deeply nested operators and run out of stack space, so we
   * just unroll the recursion when possible.
   *
   * We assume nodes are left-recursive.
   */
Method code:
765 |   private void unrollBinaryOperator(
766 |       Node n, int op, String opStr, Context context,
767 |       Context rhsContext, int leftPrecedence, int rightPrecedence) {
768 |     Node firstNonOperator = n.getFirstChild();
769 |     while (firstNonOperator.getType() == op) {
770 |       firstNonOperator = firstNonOperator.getFirstChild();
771 |     }
772 | 
773 |     addExpr(firstNonOperator, leftPrecedence, context);
774 | 
775 |     Node current = firstNonOperator;
776 |     do {
777 |       current = current.getParent();
778 |       cc.addOp(opStr, true);
779 |       addExpr(current.getFirstChild().getNext(), rightPrecedence, rhsContext);
780 |     } while (current != n);
781 |   }

===== M0557 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.shouldTraverse(NodeTraversal,Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 217-292
Documentation:

Method code:
217 |   @Override
218 |   public boolean shouldTraverse(
219 |       NodeTraversal nodeTraversal, Node n, Node parent) {
220 |     astPosition.put(n, astPositionCounter++);
221 | 
222 |     switch (n.getType()) {
223 |       case Token.FUNCTION:
224 |         if (shouldTraverseFunctions || n == cfg.getEntry().getValue()) {
225 |           exceptionHandler.push(n);
226 |           return true;
227 |         }
228 |         return false;
229 |       case Token.TRY:
230 |         exceptionHandler.push(n);
231 |         return true;
232 |     }
233 | 
234 |     /*
235 |      * We are going to stop the traversal depending on what the node's parent
236 |      * is.
237 |      *
238 |      * We are only interested in adding edges between nodes that change control
239 |      * flow. The most obvious ones are loops and IF-ELSE's. A statement
240 |      * transfers control to its next sibling.
241 |      *
242 |      * In case of an expression tree, there is no control flow within the tree
243 |      * even when there are short circuited operators and conditionals. When we
244 |      * are doing data flow analysis, we will simply synthesize lattices up the
245 |      * expression tree by finding the meet at each expression node.
246 |      *
247 |      * For example: within a Token.SWITCH, the expression in question does not
248 |      * change the control flow and need not to be considered.
249 |      */
250 |     if (parent != null) {
251 |       switch (parent.getType()) {
252 |         case Token.FOR:
253 |           // Only traverse the body of the for loop.
254 |           return n == parent.getLastChild();
255 | 
256 |         // Skip the conditions.
257 |         case Token.IF:
258 |         case Token.WHILE:
259 |         case Token.WITH:
260 |           return n != parent.getFirstChild();
261 |         case Token.DO:
262 |           return n != parent.getFirstChild().getNext();
263 |         // Only traverse the body of the cases
264 |         case Token.SWITCH:
265 |         case Token.CASE:
266 |         case Token.CATCH:
267 |         case Token.LABEL:
268 |           return n != parent.getFirstChild();
269 |         case Token.FUNCTION:
270 |           return n == parent.getFirstChild().getNext().getNext();
271 |         case Token.CONTINUE:
272 |         case Token.BREAK:
273 |         case Token.EXPR_RESULT:
274 |         case Token.VAR:
275 |         case Token.RETURN:
276 |         case Token.THROW:
277 |           return false;
278 |         case Token.TRY:
279 |           /* Just before we are about to visit the second child of the TRY node,
280 |            * we know that we will be visiting either the CATCH or the FINALLY.
281 |            * In other words, we know that the post order traversal of the TRY
282 |            * block has been finished, no more exceptions can be caught by the
283 |            * handler at this TRY block and should be taken out of the stack.
284 |            */
285 |           if (n == parent.getFirstChild().getNext()) {
286 |             Preconditions.checkState(exceptionHandler.peek() == parent);
287 |             exceptionHandler.pop();
288 |           }
289 |       }
290 |     }
291 |     return true;
292 |   }

===== M0569 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.parsing.IRFactory.TransformDispatcher.processTryStatement(TryStatement)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/parsing/IRFactory.java
Original range: 1115-1148
Documentation:

Method code:
1115 |     @Override
1116 |     Node processTryStatement(TryStatement statementNode) {
1117 |       Node node = newNode(Token.TRY,
1118 |           transformBlock(statementNode.getTryBlock()));
1119 |       Node block = newNode(Token.BLOCK);
1120 |       boolean lineSet = false;
1121 | 
1122 |       for (CatchClause cc : statementNode.getCatchClauses()) {
1123 |         // Mark the enclosing block at the same line as the first catch
1124 |         // clause.
1125 |         if (lineSet == false) {
1126 |           block.setLineno(cc.getLineno());
1127 |           maybeSetLengthFrom(block, cc);
1128 |           lineSet = true;
1129 |         }
1130 |         block.addChildToBack(transform(cc));
1131 |       }
1132 |       node.addChildToBack(block);
1133 | 
1134 |       AstNode finallyBlock = statementNode.getFinallyBlock();
1135 |       if (finallyBlock != null) {
1136 |         node.addChildToBack(transformBlock(finallyBlock));
1137 |       }
1138 | 
1139 |       // If we didn't set the line on the catch clause, then
1140 |       // we've got an empty catch clause.  Set its line to be the same
1141 |       // as the finally block (to match Old Rhino's behavior.)
1142 |       if ((lineSet == false) && (finallyBlock != null)) {
1143 |         block.setLineno(finallyBlock.getLineno());
1144 |         maybeSetLengthFrom(block, finallyBlock);
1145 |       }
1146 | 
1147 |       return node;
1148 |     }

===== M0590 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.UnreachableCodeElimination.EliminationPass.tryRemoveUnconditionalBranching(Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/UnreachableCodeElimination.java
Original range: 128-175
Documentation:
/**
     * Tries to remove n if it is an unconditional branch node (break, continue,
     * or return) and the target of n is the same as the the follow of n.
     * That is, if removing n preserves the control flow. Also if n targets
     * another unconditional branch, this function will recursively try to
     * remove the target branch as well. The reason why we want to cascade this
     * removal is because we only run this pass once. If we have code such as
     *
     * break -> break -> break
     *
     * where all 3 breaks are useless, then the order of removal matters. When
     * we first look at the first break, we see that it branches to the 2nd
     * break. However, if we remove the last break, the 2nd break becomes
     * useless and finally the first break becomes useless as well.
     *
     * @returns The target of this jump. If the target is also useless jump,
     *     the target of that useless jump recursively.
     */
Method code:
128 |     @SuppressWarnings("fallthrough")
129 |     private void tryRemoveUnconditionalBranching(Node n) {
130 |       /*
131 |        * For each unconditional branching control flow node, check to see
132 |        * if the ControlFlowAnalysis.computeFollowNode of that node is same as
133 |        * the branching target. If it is, the branch node is safe to be removed.
134 |        *
135 |        * This is not as clever as MinimizeExitPoints because it doesn't do any
136 |        * if-else conversion but it handles more complicated switch statements
137 |        * much more nicely.
138 |        */
139 | 
140 |       // If n is null the target is the end of the function, nothing to do.
141 |       if (n == null) {
142 |          return;
143 |       }
144 | 
145 |       DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> gNode = cfg.getDirectedGraphNode(n);
146 | 
147 |       if (gNode == null) {
148 |         return;
149 |       }
150 | 
151 |       switch (n.getType()) {
152 |         case Token.RETURN:
153 |           if (n.hasChildren()) {
154 |             break;
155 |           }
156 |         case Token.BREAK:
157 |         case Token.CONTINUE:
158 |           // We are looking for a control flow changing statement that always
159 |           // branches to the same node. If after removing it control still
160 |           // branches to the same node, it is safe to remove.
161 |           List<DiGraphEdge<Node, Branch>> outEdges = gNode.getOutEdges();
162 |           if (outEdges.size() == 1 &&
163 |               // If there is a next node, this jump is not useless.
164 |               (n.getNext() == null || n.getNext().isFunction())) {
165 | 
166 |             Preconditions.checkState(
167 |                 outEdges.get(0).getValue() == Branch.UNCOND);
168 |             Node fallThrough = computeFollowing(n);
169 |             Node nextCfgNode = outEdges.get(0).getDestination().getValue();
170 |             if (nextCfgNode == fallThrough) {
171 |               removeNode(n);
172 |             }
173 |           }
174 |       }
175 |     }

Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ComposeWarningsGuard.addGuard(WarningsGuard)

Original range: 86-101

Method code:
86 |   void addGuard(WarningsGuard guard) {
87 |     if (guard instanceof ComposeWarningsGuard) {
88 |       ComposeWarningsGuard composeGuard = (ComposeWarningsGuard) guard;
89 |       if (composeGuard.demoteErrors) {
90 |         this.demoteErrors = composeGuard.demoteErrors;
91 |       }
92 | 
93 |       // Reverse the guards, so that they have the same order in the result.
94 |       addGuards(Lists.newArrayList(composeGuard.guards.descendingSet()));
95 |     } else {
96 |       numberOfAdds++;
97 |       orderOfAddition.put(guard, numberOfAdds);
98 |       guards.remove(guard);
99 |       guards.add(guard);
100 |     }
101 |   }

/**
   * When the CompilerOptions and its WarningsGuard overlap, reconcile
   * any discrepencies.
   */
Method code:
313 |   protected void reconcileOptionsWithGuards() {
314 |     // DiagnosticGroups override the plain checkTypes option.
315 |     if (options.enables(DiagnosticGroups.CHECK_TYPES)) {
316 |       options.checkTypes = true;
317 |     } else if (options.disables(DiagnosticGroups.CHECK_TYPES)) {
318 |       options.checkTypes = false;
319 |     } else if (!options.checkTypes) {
320 |       // If DiagnosticGroups did not override the plain checkTypes
321 |       // option, and checkTypes is enabled, then turn off the
322 |       // parser type warnings.
323 |       options.setWarningLevel(
324 |           DiagnosticGroup.forType(
325 |               RhinoErrorReporter.TYPE_PARSE_ERROR),
326 |           CheckLevel.OFF);
327 |     }
328 | 
329 |     if (options.checkGlobalThisLevel.isOn() &&
330 |         !options.disables(DiagnosticGroups.GLOBAL_THIS)) {
331 |       options.setWarningLevel(
332 |           DiagnosticGroups.GLOBAL_THIS,
333 |           options.checkGlobalThisLevel);
334 |     }
335 | 
336 |     if (options.getLanguageIn() == LanguageMode.ECMASCRIPT5_STRICT) {
337 |       options.setWarningLevel(
338 |           DiagnosticGroups.ES5_STRICT,
339 |           CheckLevel.ERROR);
340 |     }
341 | 
342 |     // All passes must run the variable check. This synthesizes
343 |     // variables later so that the compiler doesn't crash. It also
344 |     // checks the externs file for validity. If you don't want to warn
345 |     // about missing variable declarations, we shut that specific
346 |     // error off.
347 |     if (!options.checkSymbols &&
348 |         !options.enables(DiagnosticGroups.CHECK_VARIABLES)) {
349 |       options.setWarningLevel(
350 |           DiagnosticGroups.CHECK_VARIABLES, CheckLevel.OFF);
351 |     }
352 |   }

/** Test if all characters in the string are in the Basic Latin (aka ASCII)
   * character set - that they have UTF-16 values equal to or below 0x7f.
   * This check can find which identifiers with Unicode characters need to be
   * escaped in order to allow resulting files to be processed by non-Unicode
   * aware UNIX tools and editors.
   * *
   * See http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_characters_in_Unicode
   * for more on Basic Latin.
   *
   * @param s The string to be checked for ASCII-goodness.
   *
   * @return True if all characters in the string are in Basic Latin set.
   */
Method code:
2450 |   static boolean isLatin(String s) {
2451 |     int len = s.length();
2452 |     for (int index = 0; index < len; index++) {
2453 |       char c = s.charAt(index);
2454 |       if (c > LARGEST_BASIC_LATIN) {
2455 |         return false;
2456 |       }
2457 |     }
2458 |     return true;
2459 |   }

===== M0618 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.connectToPossibleExceptionHandler(Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 830-857
Documentation:
/**
   * Connects cfgNode to the proper CATCH block if target subtree might throw
   * an exception. If there are FINALLY blocks reached before a CATCH, it will
   * make the corresponding entry in finallyMap.
   */
Method code:
830 |   private void connectToPossibleExceptionHandler(Node cfgNode, Node target) {
831 |     if (mayThrowException(target) && !exceptionHandler.isEmpty()) {
832 |       Node lastJump = cfgNode;
833 |       for (Node handler : exceptionHandler) {
834 |         if (handler.isFunction()) {
835 |           return;
836 |         }
837 |         Preconditions.checkState(handler.isTry());
838 |         Node catchBlock = NodeUtil.getCatchBlock(handler);
839 | 
840 |         if (!NodeUtil.hasCatchHandler(catchBlock)) { // No catch but a FINALLY.
841 |           if (lastJump == cfgNode) {
842 |             createEdge(cfgNode, Branch.ON_EX, handler.getLastChild());
843 |           } else {
844 |             finallyMap.put(lastJump, handler.getLastChild());
845 |           }
846 |         } else { // Has a catch.
847 |           if (lastJump == cfgNode) {
848 |             createEdge(cfgNode, Branch.ON_EX, catchBlock);
849 |             return;
850 |           } else {
851 |             finallyMap.put(lastJump, catchBlock);
852 |           }
853 |         }
854 |         lastJump = handler;
855 |       }
856 |     }
857 |   }

===== M0625 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.JSType.isSubtype(JSType)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/JSType.java
Original range: 1229-1231
Documentation:
/**
   * Checks whether {@code this} is a subtype of {@code that}.<p>
   *
   * Subtyping rules:
   * <ul>
   * <li>(unknown) &mdash; every type is a subtype of the Unknown type.</li>
   * <li>(no) &mdash; the No type is a subtype of every type.</li>
   * <li>(no-object) &mdash; the NoObject type is a subtype of every object
   * type (i.e. subtypes of the Object type).</li>
   * <li>(ref) &mdash; a type is a subtype of itself.</li>
   * <li>(union-l) &mdash; A union type is a subtype of a type U if all the
   * union type's constituents are a subtype of U. Formally<br>
   * {@code (T<sub>1</sub>, &hellip;, T<sub>n</sub>) &lt;: U} if and only
   * {@code T<sub>k</sub> &lt;: U} for all {@code k &isin; 1..n}.</li>
   * <li>(union-r) &mdash; A type U is a subtype of a union type if it is a
   * subtype of one of the union type's constituents. Formally<br>
   * {@code U &lt;: (T<sub>1</sub>, &hellip;, T<sub>n</sub>)} if and only
   * if {@code U &lt;: T<sub>k</sub>} for some index {@code k}.</li>
   * <li>(objects) &mdash; an Object {@code O<sub>1</sub>} is a subtype
   * of an object {@code O<sub>2</sub>} if it has more properties
   * than {@code O<sub>2</sub>} and all common properties are
   * pairwise subtypes.</li>
   * </ul>
   *
   * @return {@code this &lt;: that}
   */
Method code:
1229 |   public boolean isSubtype(JSType that) {
1230 |     return isSubtypeHelper(this, that);
1231 |   }

===== M0628 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.computeFollowNode(Node,Node,ControlFlowAnalysis)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 692-789
Documentation:
/**
   * Computes the follow() node of a given node and its parent. There is a side
   * effect when calling this function. If this function computed an edge that
   * exists a FINALLY, it'll attempt to connect the fromNode to the outer
   * FINALLY according to the finallyMap.
   *
   * @param fromNode The original source node since {@code node} is changed
   *        during recursion.
   * @param node The node that follow() should compute.
   */
Method code:
692 |   private static Node computeFollowNode(
693 |       Node fromNode, Node node, ControlFlowAnalysis cfa) {
694 |     /*
695 |      * This is the case where:
696 |      *
697 |      * 1. Parent is null implies that we are transferring control to the end of
698 |      * the script.
699 |      *
700 |      * 2. Parent is a function implies that we are transferring control back to
701 |      * the caller of the function.
702 |      *
703 |      * 3. If the node is a return statement, we should also transfer control
704 |      * back to the caller of the function.
705 |      *
706 |      * 4. If the node is root then we have reached the end of what we have been
707 |      * asked to traverse.
708 |      *
709 |      * In all cases we should transfer control to a "symbolic return" node.
710 |      * This will make life easier for DFAs.
711 |      */
712 |     Node parent = node.getParent();
713 |     if (parent == null || parent.isFunction() ||
714 |         (cfa != null && node == cfa.root)) {
715 |       return null;
716 |     }
717 | 
718 |     // If we are just before a IF/WHILE/DO/FOR:
719 |     switch (parent.getType()) {
720 |       // The follow() of any of the path from IF would be what follows IF.
721 |       case Token.IF:
722 |         return computeFollowNode(fromNode, parent, cfa);
723 |       case Token.CASE:
724 |       case Token.DEFAULT_CASE:
725 |         // After the body of a CASE, the control goes to the body of the next
726 |         // case, without having to go to the case condition.
727 |         if (parent.getNext() != null) {
728 |           if (parent.getNext().isCase()) {
729 |             return parent.getNext().getFirstChild().getNext();
730 |           } else if (parent.getNext().isDefaultCase()) {
731 |             return parent.getNext().getFirstChild();
732 |           } else {
733 |             Preconditions.checkState(false, "Not reachable");
734 |           }
735 |         } else {
736 |           return computeFollowNode(fromNode, parent, cfa);
737 |         }
738 |         break;
739 |       case Token.FOR:
740 |         if (NodeUtil.isForIn(parent)) {
741 |           return parent;
742 |         } else {
743 |           return parent.getFirstChild().getNext().getNext();
744 |         }
745 |       case Token.WHILE:
746 |       case Token.DO:
747 |         return parent;
748 |       case Token.TRY:
749 |         // If we are coming out of the TRY block...
750 |         if (parent.getFirstChild() == node) {
751 |           if (NodeUtil.hasFinally(parent)) { // and have FINALLY block.
752 |             return computeFallThrough(parent.getLastChild());
753 |           } else { // and have no FINALLY.
754 |             return computeFollowNode(fromNode, parent, cfa);
755 |           }
756 |         // CATCH block.
757 |         } else if (NodeUtil.getCatchBlock(parent) == node){
758 |           if (NodeUtil.hasFinally(parent)) { // and have FINALLY block.
759 |             return computeFallThrough(node.getNext());
760 |           } else {
761 |             return computeFollowNode(fromNode, parent, cfa);
762 |           }
763 |         // If we are coming out of the FINALLY block...
764 |         } else if (parent.getLastChild() == node){
765 |           if (cfa != null) {
766 |             for (Node finallyNode : cfa.finallyMap.get(parent)) {
767 |               cfa.createEdge(fromNode, Branch.ON_EX, finallyNode);
768 |             }
769 |           }
770 |           return computeFollowNode(fromNode, parent, cfa);
771 |         }
772 |     }
773 | 
774 |     // Now that we are done with the special cases follow should be its
775 |     // immediate sibling, unless its sibling is a function
776 |     Node nextSibling = node.getNext();
777 | 
778 |     // Skip function declarations because control doesn't get pass into it.
779 |     while (nextSibling != null && nextSibling.isFunction()) {
780 |       nextSibling = nextSibling.getNext();
781 |     }
782 | 
783 |     if (nextSibling != null) {
784 |       return computeFallThrough(nextSibling);
785 |     } else {
786 |       // If there are no more siblings, control is transferred up the AST.
787 |       return computeFollowNode(fromNode, parent, cfa);
788 |     }
789 |   }

===== M0635 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.parsing.IRFactory.TransformDispatcher.parseDirectives(Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/parsing/IRFactory.java
Original range: 598-613
Documentation:
/**
     * Parse the directives, encode them in the AST, and remove their nodes.
     *
     * For information on ES5 directives, see section 14.1 of
     * ECMA-262, Edition 5.
     *
     * It would be nice if Rhino would eventually take care of this for
     * us, but right now their directive-processing is a one-off.
     */
Method code:
598 |     private void parseDirectives(Node node) {
599 |       // Remove all the directives, and encode them in the AST.
600 |       Set<String> directives = null;
601 |       while (isDirective(node.getFirstChild())) {
602 |         String directive = node.removeFirstChild().getFirstChild().getString();
603 |         if (directives == null) {
604 |           directives = Sets.newHashSet(directive);
605 |         } else {
606 |           directives.add(directive);
607 |         }
608 |       }
609 | 
610 |       if (directives != null) {
611 |         node.setDirectives(directives);
612 |       }
613 |     }

===== M0650 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.Compiler.parseInputs()
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/Compiler.java
Original range: 1300-1420
Documentation:
/**
   * Parses the externs and main inputs.
   *
   * @return A synthetic root node whose two children are the externs root
   *     and the main root
   */
Method code:
1300 |   Node parseInputs() {
1301 |     boolean devMode = options.devMode != DevMode.OFF;
1302 | 
1303 |     // If old roots exist (we are parsing a second time), detach each of the
1304 |     // individual file parse trees.
1305 |     if (externsRoot != null) {
1306 |       externsRoot.detachChildren();
1307 |     }
1308 |     if (jsRoot != null) {
1309 |       jsRoot.detachChildren();
1310 |     }
1311 | 
1312 |     // Parse main JS sources.
1313 |     jsRoot = IR.block();
1314 |     jsRoot.setIsSyntheticBlock(true);
1315 | 
1316 |     externsRoot = IR.block();
1317 |     externsRoot.setIsSyntheticBlock(true);
1318 | 
1319 |     externAndJsRoot = IR.block(externsRoot, jsRoot);
1320 |     externAndJsRoot.setIsSyntheticBlock(true);
1321 | 
1322 |     if (options.tracer.isOn()) {
1323 |       tracker = new PerformanceTracker(jsRoot, options.tracer);
1324 |       addChangeHandler(tracker.getCodeChangeHandler());
1325 |     }
1326 | 
1327 |     Tracer tracer = newTracer(PARSING_PASS_NAME);
1328 | 
1329 |     try {
1330 |       // Parse externs sources.
1331 |       for (CompilerInput input : externs) {
1332 |         Node n = input.getAstRoot(this);
1333 |         if (hasErrors()) {
1334 |           return null;
1335 |         }
1336 |         externsRoot.addChildToBack(n);
1337 |       }
1338 | 
1339 |       // Modules inferred in ProcessCommonJS pass.
1340 |       if (options.transformAMDToCJSModules || options.processCommonJSModules) {
1341 |         processAMDAndCommonJSModules();
1342 |       }
1343 | 
1344 |       hoistExterns(externsRoot);
1345 | 
1346 |       // Check if the sources need to be re-ordered.
1347 |       boolean staleInputs = false;
1348 |       if (options.dependencyOptions.needsManagement()) {
1349 |         for (CompilerInput input : inputs) {
1350 |           // Forward-declare all the provided types, so that they
1351 |           // are not flagged even if they are dropped from the process.
1352 |           for (String provide : input.getProvides()) {
1353 |             getTypeRegistry().forwardDeclareType(provide);
1354 |           }
1355 |         }
1356 | 
1357 |         try {
1358 |           inputs =
1359 |               (moduleGraph == null ? new JSModuleGraph(modules) : moduleGraph)
1360 |               .manageDependencies(options.dependencyOptions, inputs);
1361 |           staleInputs = true;
1362 |         } catch (CircularDependencyException e) {
1363 |           report(JSError.make(
1364 |               JSModule.CIRCULAR_DEPENDENCY_ERROR, e.getMessage()));
1365 |         } catch (MissingProvideException e) {
1366 |           report(JSError.make(
1367 |               MISSING_ENTRY_ERROR, e.getMessage()));
1368 |         } catch (JSModuleGraph.MissingModuleException e) {
1369 |           report(JSError.make(
1370 |               MISSING_MODULE_ERROR, e.getMessage()));
1371 |         }
1372 | 
1373 |         // If in IDE mode, we ignore the error and keep going.
1374 |         if (hasErrors()) {
1375 |           return null;
1376 |         }
1377 |       }
1378 | 
1379 |       hoistNoCompileFiles();
1380 | 
1381 |       if (staleInputs) {
1382 |         repartitionInputs();
1383 |       }
1384 | 
1385 |       // Build the AST.
1386 |       for (CompilerInput input : inputs) {
1387 |         Node n = input.getAstRoot(this);
1388 |         if (n == null) {
1389 |           continue;
1390 |         }
1391 | 
1392 |         if (devMode) {
1393 |           runSanityCheck();
1394 |           if (hasErrors()) {
1395 |             return null;
1396 |           }
1397 |         }
1398 | 
1399 |         if (options.sourceMapOutputPath != null ||
1400 |             options.nameReferenceReportPath != null) {
1401 | 
1402 |           // Annotate the nodes in the tree with information from the
1403 |           // input file. This information is used to construct the SourceMap.
1404 |           SourceInformationAnnotator sia =
1405 |               new SourceInformationAnnotator(
1406 |                   input.getName(), options.devMode != DevMode.OFF);
1407 |           NodeTraversal.traverse(this, n, sia);
1408 |         }
1409 | 
1410 |         jsRoot.addChildToBack(n);
1411 |       }
1412 | 
1413 |       if (hasErrors()) {
1414 |         return null;
1415 |       }
1416 |       return externAndJsRoot;
1417 |     } finally {
1418 |       stopTracer(tracer, PARSING_PASS_NAME);
1419 |     }
1420 |   }

     * This may start a new line if the current line is longer than the line
     * length threshold.
     */
Method code:
490 |     @Override
491 |     void maybeCutLine() {
492 |       if (lineLength > lineLengthThreshold) {
493 |         // Use the preferred position provided it will break the line.
494 |         if (preferredBreakPosition > lineStartPosition &&
495 |             preferredBreakPosition < lineStartPosition + lineLength) {
496 |           int position = preferredBreakPosition;
497 |           code.insert(position, '\n');
498 |           prevCutPosition = position;
499 |           reportLineCut(lineIndex, position - lineStartPosition, true);
500 |           lineIndex++;
501 |           lineLength -= (position - lineStartPosition);
502 |           lineStartPosition = position + 1;
503 |         } else {
504 |           startNewLine();
505 |         }
506 |       }
507 |     }

===== M0664 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.JSTypeRegistry.addReferenceTypeIndexedByProperty(String,JSType)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/JSTypeRegistry.java
Original range: 686-705
Documentation:

Method code:
686 |   private void addReferenceTypeIndexedByProperty(
687 |       String propertyName, JSType type) {
688 |     if (type instanceof ObjectType && ((ObjectType) type).hasReferenceName()) {
689 |       Map<String, ObjectType> typeSet =
690 |           eachRefTypeIndexedByProperty.get(propertyName);
691 |       if (typeSet == null) {
692 |         typeSet = Maps.newHashMap();
693 |         eachRefTypeIndexedByProperty.put(propertyName, typeSet);
694 |       }
695 |       ObjectType objType = (ObjectType) type;
696 |       typeSet.put(objType.getReferenceName(), objType);
697 |     } else if (type instanceof NamedType) {
698 |       addReferenceTypeIndexedByProperty(
699 |           propertyName, ((NamedType) type).getReferencedType());
700 |     } else if (type.isUnionType()) {
701 |       for (JSType alternate : type.toMaybeUnionType().getAlternates()) {
702 |         addReferenceTypeIndexedByProperty(propertyName, alternate);
703 |       }
704 |     }
705 |   }

Method code:
514 |     @Override
515 |     void endFile() {
516 |       super.endFile();
517 |       if (!preferLineBreakAtEndOfFile) {
518 |         return;
519 |       }
520 |       if (lineLength > lineLengthThreshold / 2) {
521 |         // Add an extra break at end of file.
522 |         append(";");
523 |         startNewLine();
524 |       } else if (prevCutPosition > 0) {
525 |         // Shift the previous break to end of file by replacing it with a
526 |         // <space> and adding a new break at end of file. Adding the space
527 |         // handles cases like instanceof\nfoo. (it would be nice to avoid this)
528 |         code.setCharAt(prevCutPosition, ' ');
529 |         lineStartPosition = prevLineStartPosition;
530 |         lineLength = code.length() - lineStartPosition;
531 |         reportLineCut(lineIndex, prevCutPosition + 1, false);
532 |         lineIndex--;
533 |         prevCutPosition = 0;
534 |         prevLineStartPosition = 0;
535 |         append(";");
536 |         startNewLine();
537 |       } else {
538 |         // A small file with no line breaks. We do nothing in this case to
539 |         // avoid excessive line breaks. It's not ideal if a lot of these pile
540 |         // up, but that is reasonably unlikely.
541 |       }
542 |     }

Documentation:
/**
   * Tells the type system that {@code owner} may have a property named
   * {@code propertyName}. This allows the registry to keep track of what
   * types a property is defined upon.
   *
   * This is NOT the same as saying that {@code owner} must have a property
   * named type. ObjectType#hasProperty attempts to minimize false positives
   * ("if we're not sure, then don't type check this property"). The type
   * registry, on the other hand, should attempt to minimize false negatives
   * ("if this property is assigned anywhere in the program, it must
   * show up in the type registry").
   */
Method code:
672 |   public void registerPropertyOnType(String propertyName, JSType type) {
673 |     UnionTypeBuilder typeSet = typesIndexedByProperty.get(propertyName);
674 |     if (typeSet == null) {
675 |       typeSet = new UnionTypeBuilder(this, PROPERTY_CHECKING_UNION_SIZE);
676 |       typesIndexedByProperty.put(propertyName, typeSet);
677 |     }
678 | 
679 |     typeSet.addAlternate(type);
680 |     addReferenceTypeIndexedByProperty(propertyName, type);
681 | 
682 |     // Clear cached values that depend on typesIndexedByProperty.
683 |     greatestSubtypeByProperty.remove(propertyName);
684 |   }

===== M0691 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.FunctionType.setPrototypeBasedOn(ObjectType,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/FunctionType.java
Original range: 399-425
Documentation:

Method code:
399 |   void setPrototypeBasedOn(ObjectType baseType, Node propertyNode) {
400 |     // This is a bit weird. We need to successfully handle these
401 |     // two cases:
402 |     // Foo.prototype = new Bar();
403 |     // and
404 |     // Foo.prototype = {baz: 3};
405 |     // In the first case, we do not want new properties to get
406 |     // added to Bar. In the second case, we do want new properties
407 |     // to get added to the type of the anonymous object.
408 |     //
409 |     // We handle this by breaking it into two cases:
410 |     //
411 |     // In the first case, we create a new PrototypeObjectType and set
412 |     // its implicit prototype to the type being assigned. This ensures
413 |     // that Bar will not get any properties of Foo.prototype, but properties
414 |     // later assigned to Bar will get inherited properly.
415 |     //
416 |     // In the second case, we just use the anonymous object as the prototype.
417 |     if (baseType.hasReferenceName() ||
418 |         isNativeObjectType() ||
419 |         baseType.isFunctionPrototypeType()) {
420 |       typeOfThis.extendTemplateTypeMap(baseType.getTemplateTypeMap());
421 |       baseType = new PrototypeObjectType(
422 |           registry, getReferenceName() + ".prototype", baseType);
423 |     }
424 |     setPrototype(baseType, propertyNode);
425 |   }
